みずほ経済 EXPRESS

みずほ GDP ナウ（2026 年 10 月中旬時点） 
～7～9 月期 GDP は前期比＋0.03％（年率＋0.10％）と推計～

調査部  シニア日本経済エコノミスト 越山祐資 
同             エコノミスト  阿部大樹 
080-1069-4835 yusuke.koshiyama@mizuho-rt.co.jp 
■ 10 月中旬時点の「みずほ GDP ナウ」は 7～9 月期 GDP を前期比＋0.10％と予測 
みずほ総合研究所では景気動向をタイムリーに把握するべく、浦沢（2023）等を参考にGDPナウキ
ャスティングに取り組んできた。これを「みずほGDPナウ」と呼称することとし、太田他（2024）で
はデータドリブン予測である「みずほGDPナウ」が民間予測平均並みの予測精度を確保できることを
示した。その上で、酒井他（2024）をはじめとして、月次経済指標を用いたGDPナウキャスティング
の結果を紹介してきたところである。 
本稿では10月中旬時点までに得られる月次経済指標を用い、 「みずほGDPナウ」 による7～9月期GDP
の予測結果を紹介する。予測に用いたデータは、8月分の鉱工業生産、 消費活動指数、 所定外労働時間、
消費財出荷指数、第3次産業活動指数、9月分の中小企業景況調査（売上げ見通しDI）である1。図表1
には、予測に使用した各月次経済指標の推移を示している。 
図表 1 各月次経済指標の推移

（注）青字は改善、赤字は悪化を示す

日本銀行「消費活動指数」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 2

2

モデルによる10月中旬時点における7 ～9月期実質GDP成長率の予測値は、図表2のとおり前期比＋
0.03％ （年率＋0.10％）となった。 他機関による予測では、東京財団政策研究所による10月15日時点の
ナウキャスティングが同＋0.29％（年率＋1.18％）、日本経済研究センターが公表した10月のESPフォ
ーキャスト調査（回答期間：2026年9月30日～10月7日、回答者37名）における民間予測値平均が前期
比年率＋0.08％となっている。「みずほGDPナウ」の予測値は、民間コンセンサスよりやや高いもの
の、概ね近い伸びを予測していると言える。 
なお、多くの民間エコノミストが保守的な見方をしている理由の一つには、GDP統計作成上のテク
ニカルな要因がある。4～6月期には、 中東情勢悪化を背景に原油 ・ ナフサ輸入が大幅に減少したほか、
石油化学製品の供給途絶懸念から幅広い企業で在庫積み増しの動きが生じた。やや直感に反するが、
いずれもGDPに対しては押し上げ要因となる。 そのため、4～6月期GDPは前期比＋0.36％ （年率＋1.45％）
とプラス成長になったものの、 成長率が示すほど景気の実態は強くなかったとみるのが自然であろう。
反対に、7～9月期については、4～6月期の反動で輸入増・在庫減の動きが顕在化し、GDPには一定の
下方圧力がかかる可能性がある。みずほ総合研究所が9月に公表したレポート2でも、こうしたテクニ
カル要因を考慮の上、7～9月期GDPは横ばい圏の伸びにとどまると予想している。 
■ 「みずほ GDP ナウ」による予測値下方修正の主因は生産・消費関連指標の悪化 
「みずほGDPナウ」 による7～9月期のGDP（1次速報値） の予測にあたっては10月中旬までに公表さ
れた月次経済指標を使って予測値を4回更新した。図表3では、予測値の更新過程と各月次経済指標の
寄与度を示している3。予測値が下方修正された主因は、8月の鉱工業生産、 消費財出荷指数の低下だ。
特に鉱工業生産指数については、7月末に発生した熊本地震による影響が下押し要因になったとみら
れる。 大手自動車メーカーで工場の操業が停止した影響で自動車が前月比▲6.8％と大幅に減産したほ
か、 電子部品 ・ デバイスも同▲1.9％の減産となった。7月調査時点の生産予測指数は前月比＋3.1％（ 補
図表 2 各機関の実質GDP成長率予測値 図表 3 予測値の改定過程と各経済指標の寄与度

### Page 3

3

正値ベース）と増産が見込まれていたが、大幅に下振れた格好だ。一方、世界的に旺盛なAI・データ
センター関連需要を背景に、半導体製造装置を含む生産用機械などが増産したものの、熊本地震の影
響を打ち返せなかった。消費財出荷指数の低下については、4～5月の耐久財特需が一巡したことで反
動減の動きが生じた。非耐久財の出荷は前月から増加したものの、7月の落ち込みも踏まえると、 基調
としては力強さを欠いていると言えよう。長雨や低温、といった天候不順により生鮮野菜価格が上昇
しているほか、食肉も値上がりが大きく、こうした手に取る機会が多い品目の価格上昇が家計の節約
志向を強め、個人消費の下押し圧力となっているとみられる。9月についても、食品のほか、原油・石
油化学製品等のコスト高の影響で幅広い品目の価格上昇が加速する見込みであり、個人消費は冴えな
い動きが続く可能性がある。 
以上のように、7～9月期成長率は低い伸びに抑えられる公算が大きい。前述のとおり原油・ナフサ
の輸入増、 企業の在庫調整といった動きはGDP統計上、成長率に対してマイナスに寄与する。加えて、
熊本地震による生産減や天候要因による消費支出抑制の動きが下押し要因になる見込みだ。 
次回の 「みずほGDPナウ」については、11月16日に公表される7～9月期GDP1次速報を挟んで、10～
12月期GDPのリアルタイム予測に取り組む。10月分の鉱工業生産や消費活動指数の結果等を踏まえて
推計し、12月中旬頃のレポート発刊を予定している。原油高等を受けた物価上昇圧力の強まりや堅調
なAI・データセンター関連需要など、プラス・マイナス双方の影響が交錯する中、ナウキャストによ
る景気動向の把握はより重要なものとなるだろう。

～DFMを用いた日本のGDPナウキャスティング～」 、 みずほリサーチ＆テクノロジーズ 『みずほ

1 ステップワイズ法により使用データを採択した。詳細は太田ほか（2024）を参照されたい。今後のモデルの予測精度のパフォ
ーマンス評価等を踏まえ、採択するデータについては見直しを行う可能性がある。 
2 詳細については、服部直樹「年率＋1.4％と 1 次速報から小幅に上方修正（4～6 月期2 次QE）」（2026 年9 月8 日）を参照され
たい。 
3 太田他（2024）で示した枠組みと同様、図表 2 の折れ線が各時点における実質GDP 成長率の予測値であり、月次の経済指標が
新たに公表されたり改定されたりすることで予測値が更新される。棒グラフは、予測値の改定幅、すなわち前回予測との差を各
月次経済指標で寄与度分解したもので、寄与度を合計するとモデル予測値の改定幅と一致する。なお、 「みずほGDP ナウ」は各
月次経済指標についても公表前に予測値を計算しており、各指標の寄与度はモデル予測値と実績値の乖離から計算されることか
ら、寄与度の符号は経済指標の変化の方向とは必ずしも一致しない。

作者: 
中诚信国际 研究院

价格数据边际改善释放何种信号？，2026
年9 月9 日

如何理解价格数据的回落？，2026 年 8 月
9 日

PPI 环比转负，下半年物价表现或回归基
本面，2026 年7 月9 日

如何看待PPI-CPI 剪刀差走扩？ 
        ——2026 年9 月价格数据点评

事件：2026 年 9 月全国居民消费价格指数（CPI）同比 0.9%（前
值 0.8%），环比 0.2%（前值 0.4%），1-9 月 CPI 同比 0.9%（前值
0.9%）；核心 CPI 同比 1%（前值 1%），环比0（前值 0.1%），1-9 月
核心 CPI 同比 1.1%（前值 1.1%）；工业生产者出厂价格指数（PPI）
同比5%（前值3.8%），环比1.2%（前值0.4%），1-9 月PPI 同比2.3%
（前值2%）。 
受油价、芯片等涨价影响，CPI 环比连续两个月为正,9 月略高于
季节性水平，CPI 同比涨幅较上月小幅扩大；1-9 月，CPI 同比及核心
CPI 同比均较上年底 回升但仍低于上半年水平 ，价格持续修复 但修复
节奏放缓。9 月 CPI 同比增长 0.9%，较上月回升 0.1 个百分点，连续
两个月回升，环比延续正增长， 为 0.2%，强于季节性水平（2023 年-
2025 年，9 月 CPI 环比均值为 0.1%）。9 月核心 CPI（剔除食品和能
源）同比增长 1%，与上月持平，仍位于较低水平， 环比 0，持平季节
性水平（2023 年-2025 年，9 月核心CPI 环比均值为0）。1-9 月，CPI
同比及核心 CPI 同比分别为 0.9%和 1.1%，均与 1-8 月持平，较上年底
和上年同期水平回升，但均较上半年回落 0.1 个百分点，仍位于较低
区间水平，价格修复的基础有待进一步夯实。 
 油价仍是 CPI 环比维持正增的重要支撑， 同时受成本走高影响， 本
月移动电话机、计算机、数据存储设备和平板电脑 价格环比上涨、
对CPI 环比维持正增带来一定助力。工业消费品中，受国际市场价格
变动影响，9 月国内汽油价格环比上涨4.9%，连续两个月环比正增，
油价仍是CPI 环比维持正增的重要支撑。受芯片等成本上涨影响，从
7 月开始，多家手机厂商已经陆续上调部分在售产品价格。9 月 1 日，
www.ccxi.com.cn

2026 年9 月价格数据点评  
如何看待PPI-CPI 剪刀差走扩？ 
华为、小米、荣耀多款手机官方调整售价，单次涨幅从 200 元至 1000 元不等。9 月移动电话
机、计算机、数据存储设备和平板电脑价格环比分别上涨 3.7%、2.0%、1.7%和 1.5%，合计影
响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.04 个百分点。此外，秋装上市，带动服装价格环比上涨 0.7%，影响 CPI
环比上涨约0.03 个百分点。 
 暑期效应消退，服务价格环比由涨转降，持平季节性水平，对 CPI 环比的影响由上月支撑转
为拖累，其中飞机票、宾馆住宿等 假期出行相关服务价格环比均由正转负。 9 月，服务价格
同比上涨0.8%，涨幅与上月相同，服务价格环比由上月上涨0.1%转为下降0.2%，持平季节性
（2023-2025 年9 月，服务价格环比均值为-0.2%），对CPI 环比的影响由上月上拉约0.04 个
百分点转为下拉约 0.08 个百分点。具体来看，随着暑假结束，飞机票、宾馆住宿环比均由正
转负，飞机票、宾馆住宿和旅行社收费价格环比分别下降 8.7%、7.0%和 6.0%，合计影响 CPI
环比下降约0.12 个百分点。 
 食品价格环比维持正增长 ，但低于季节性水平 ，同比降幅边际扩大，食品价格仍偏弱 。9 月
食品价格同比下降 1.7%，降幅比上月扩大 0.3 个百分点，影响 CPI 同比下降约 0.29 个百分
点，环比上涨0.4%，连续两个月正增长，影响CPI 环比上涨约0.06 个百分点，但仍低于季节
性水平（2023-2025 年 9 月，食品价格环比均值为 0.6%）。其中，受猪肉供给结构调整以及
暑假、中秋和国庆备货需求带动，鲜菜及猪肉价格环比连续 3 个月为正。此外，休渔期结束，
水产品价格环比连续两个月为负。

如何看待PPI-CPI 剪刀差走扩？ 
受输入性因素、 上游原材料行业供需关系调整 、人工智能行业快速发展提振相关行业需
求等带动，9 月PPI 环比连续两个月正增长，同比5%、升至2022 年7 月以来高点。1-9 月PPI
同比2.3%，较上半年上行0.8 个百分点，为2023 年以来的高点。9 月PPI 同比5%，较上月上
行 1.2 个百分点，PPI 环比连续两个月维持正增，9 月环比上涨 1.2%。PPI 分项中，生产资料
价格同比涨幅较上月扩大 1.5 个百分点，为 6.5%，是 PPI 同比上行的重要支撑，环比连续两
个月正增，为1.4%；生活资料价格同比降幅连续3 个月收窄，9 月较上月收窄0.4 个百分点，
为-0.1%，环比连续两个月正增，9 月为0.1%。 
 受中东局势与全球 AI 快速发展影响，国际油价、有色等大宗商品价格上涨带动国内相关行业
价格上涨，是 9 月 PPI 环比上行最重要的支撑；同时行业供需结构调整叠加季节性因素带动
煤炭、非金属矿物制品等部分行业价格上涨；人工智能快速发展，AI 产业链部分需求增加、
价格上涨，上述因素均对 PPI 形成支撑。从行业来看，受原油、有色金属等海外定价大宗商

如何看待PPI-CPI 剪刀差走扩？ 
品价格上行影响，石油开采、精炼石油产品制造、有机化学原料制造价格环比分别上涨14.5%、
7.4%和5.4%，有色金属矿采选业、有色金属冶炼和压延加工业价格环比分别上涨1.6%和1.5%，
5 个行业合计影响PPI 环比上涨约0.63 个百分点，是本月PPI 环比改善最重要的支撑。同时，
受冬季供暖备煤需求增加、主产矿区供应阶段性偏紧 等因素影响，煤炭开采和洗选业、煤炭
加工价格环比分别上涨9.5%和13.6%。9 月是建筑施工旺季，钢材、水泥等建筑材料需求有所
回升，加之 煤炭等原材料成本上涨，非金属矿物制品业 （主要包括水泥、玻璃等） 价格环比
由负转正，上涨0.3%，黑色金属冶炼和压延加工业价格环比上涨0.2%，4 个行业合计影响PPI
环比上涨约 0.38 个百分点。此外，人工智能、算电协同等领域加快发展，电气机械和器材制
造业、计算机通信和其他电子设备制造业等 AI 产业链相关行业价格环比均上涨 0.4%，亦对
PPI 上行带来支撑。  
 生活资料价格环比连续两个月为正但仍位于低位区间，同比降幅收窄至 0 附近。生活资料价
格同比降幅连续 3 个月收窄但仍为负，9 月同比下降 0.1%，环比延续 8 月的正增长态势、连
续两个月为正，不过仍位于0 左右的低位区间，终端需求仍偏弱。

如何看待PPI-CPI 剪刀差走扩？

整体来看，今年以来物价整体呈现温和修复态势，呈现以下特征：一是上下游景气度存
在温差，中下游尤其下游中小企业仍在承受成本挤压 。今年以来，生产资料与生活资料同比
分化走势逐渐加大，二者剪刀差由年初 1 月的 0.4 个百分点扩大到 9 月的 6.6 个百分点。结
合 PMI 数据来看，截至 9 月，主要原材料购进价格分项与出厂价格剪刀差连续两个月扩大。
涨价能力和利润持续向上游资源行业集中，终端消费品价格整体仍偏弱，中下游制造业面临
成本挤压。二是 AI 产业链涨价正逐步成为影响物价走势的新增变量。从电气机械、算力设
施等上游环节，到存储器件、平板终端、手机等下游消费品，AI 相关产品价格表现均较好，
成为价格修复的重要力量。三是当前经济仍面临供给端涨价与需求端疲软并存的矛盾，PPI-
CPI 剪刀差扩大。自今年 4 月 PPI 同比首次超过 CPI 同比以来，PPI-CPI 正剪刀差由 4 月的
1.6%波动扩大至 9 月的 4.1%，9 月较 8 月扩大 1.1 个百分点。供需失衡的格局仍在延续，价
格修复仍受到掣肘。 
后续来看，9 月以来中东局势再度升级，国际原油价格波动上行，截至10 月13 日，WTI
原油期货结算价由 9 月底的 90.42 美元/桶，波动上行至 94.1 美元/桶。若油价持续高位运
行，短期石化产业链价格仍将位于较高水平， PPI 同比保持韧性仍有支撑，并沿产业链向
CPI 能源分项传导。但也要看到，当前生产端价格改善仍主要集中于能源、有色和部分新动
能行业，终端需求相关行业价格仍偏弱，核心CPI 始终在1%左右的较低水平徘徊，全面物价
上涨的可能性较低，完成2%的年度通胀目标难度仍较大。

### Page 3

TSMC (2330.TW)
15 October 2026 Citi Research
3
Rather than now slowing down, it’s actually getting stronger
TSMC reported an upbeat 3Q26 result with robust 4Q26 outlook. It guided for
4Q26 revenue of US$52.3–54.3 billion, representing about 14% QoQ growth and
58% year-over-year growth at the midpoint (based on FX at NT$31.8 per USD).
4Q26 GM/OPM is expected at 65–67% and 56.5–58.5%. TSMC reiterated its 2026
capex to be US$60–64 billion and said investment from 2026 to 2028 will be

significantly higher than in the previous three years, reflecting strong multi-year
customer demand, including agentic AI. It is also worth noting that TSMC plans to
begin using the High-NA EUV technology around 2030 and develop a 12-inch
mask capability by 2031, when it expects the tools to offer sufficient maturity and
cost effectiveness. Management said this timing does not undermine its
confidence in technology leadership.
Management described AI-related demand as exceptionally strong, supported by

accelerators, networking chips, and rising demand for CPUs associated with
agentic AI. TSMC’s previously guided AI GPU/accelerator CAGR to be mid-to-high
50%, but now sees more upside ahead. Given rising concern on AI expanding
peaking out, the management said it is clear that its capacity planning strategy has
considered customer forecasts, data-center construction, power availability, and
other supply-chain constraints. Management still judges demand to be above

available supply and is working to close the gap, while emphasizing disciplined
planning to limit the risk of overbuilding.
Figure 1. TSMC – 3Q26 results comparison
© 2026 Citigroup Inc. No redistribution without Citigroup’s written permission.
Source: Citi Research, Bloomberg
Capacity expansion plan and allocation
During the earnings call, TSMC also mentioned that despite the tight capacity
supply, customers cannot generally sublet or auction wafer capacity, while there is

limited case that collaboration is through mask-license agreements. For the
advanced packaging, TSMC has announced its Global Foundries and VIS’
agreement on interposer as part of its backend supply strategy, intended to help
ensure customers have enough capacity to bring products to market; It also
reiterated that TSMC’s CoWoS remains as the leading customer’s choice and is
already in volume production at 5x reticle size, while it is developing 9x and larger

solutions and evaluating CoPoS.

### Page 4

TSMC (2330.TW)
15 October 2026 Citi Research
4
Reiterate Buy, best position in AI arena
Our earnings projection is largely unchanged for 2027 /2028 earnings. While its
capex expansion, overseas operation and N2 will likely be a margin dilution factor
initially, due to its cross-node leverage, high UTR and yield rate, we believe its GM
downside is manageable. Overall, we see strong advanced node demand into next
year and the long-term order visibility will be supported for higher capex cycle.  We

maintain our TP to NT$4,000, based on unchanged 25x of 2027 EPS estimate;
reiterate Buy.
Key TSMC earnings call recap
n4Q26 guidance
–Sales: US$52.3-54.3bn (+14% QoQ at the midpoint)
–GPM: 65-67% (assuming USDTWD=31.8)
–OPM: 56.5-58.5%
n2026 full-year & long-term guidance
–Full year sales: raise 2026 full-year USD-based sales guidance from more
than 40% growth to +44% growth
–Full year capex: retain its 2026 capex guidance at US$60-64bn (70-80%
advanced process, 10% specialty, 10-20% for advanced packaging, testing

and masks)
–GM outlook
•4Q GM to be impacted by N2 and overseas fab ramps, but partially
supported by productivity improvement, UTR enhancement and
optimizing capacity allocation
•GM dilution from overseas fab would be c2ppts impact for the full-year
2026; with more new capacity expansion in the US, the margin dilution
impact would widen to 3-4ppts in the next few years.
–Long-term outlook
•Retains 5-year AI sales CAGR (2024-2029) remains at high-50% or above

nCapacity planning
–TSMC currently has 20 fabs in total. It plans to add 13 fabs in Taiwan and add
5-6 overseas fabs.
–N2 capacity will double in 2027; it has also started preparing for A14 capacity
with volume production starting from 2028.
–US Arizona fab 2 construction is complete and begin volume production in
3Q27, ahead of schedule.
–JP fab 2 would produce N3 with mass production from 2028

### Page 10

TSMC (2330.TW)
15 October 2026 Citi Research
10
TSMC
Company description
TSMC is the founder and leader of the dedicated IC foundry segment. The
company has built its reputation by offering advanced and "more-than-
Moore" wafer production processes and industry-leading manufacturing
efficiency. From its inception in 1987, TSMC has consistently offered the
foundry segment's leading technologies and TSMC-compatible design
services.
Investment strategy

We rate TSMC shares as Buy. We expect TSMC to deliver sales growth of
>40% in recent years thanks to strong growth from HPC/AI as well as stable
demand growth from smartphone, Io T, and automotive on tech migration and
a shortened replacment cycle. CPU outsourcing to TSMC offers further
growth potential. A rising cash dividend should also support the share price.
While TSMC has been increasing its capex meaningfully since 2009, its cost
structure remaisn competitive through continuous product mix

improvements, efficiency enhancements, and cost reductions.
Valuation
Our target price for TSMC shares of NT$4,000 is based on 25x our 2027E
EPS, higher than its three-year average forward PER, versus global peers’
average of 16-25x. We believe our target PER is justified by TSMC's leading
position in advanced process node and robust AI demand growth outlook.
Given its continuing leadership in the semiconductor foundry industry, we
believe TSMC’s stable order visibility and earnings outlook make it less

vulnerable to a potential global economic downturn than some of its peers.
Our target price equates to 2026E/27E P/E of ~ 36x/25x and 2026E/27E P/B of
~13x/10x.
Risks
Key downside risks that could impede the shares from achieving our target
price include: 1) weakness in the global semiconductor market; 2) larger-
than-expected margin contractions due to depreciation cost increases and
strong NTD; 3) competitors entering the foundry business; 4) longer-than-

expected digestion of inventory in the supply chain; and 5) a slowdown in
demand either due to a global economic downturn or a global trade
disruption triggered by tariffs.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

Quanta (2382.TW): AI servers ramp up to drive growth ahead; model 
transition and PC shipment slowdown could weigh on 4Q26; Neutral
19 October 2026 | 1:57AM HKT
Quanta Sep revenues declined by 23% MoM, which we attributed to AI servers model 
transition, while PC entering quarter-end at strong MoM of +62% MoM. Looking 
forward, we expect monthly revenues to resume sequential growth in Oct to Dec, 
riding on reducing impact of AI servers model transition as new racks ramping up, 
along with growing general servers on agentic AI, and ASP increase on better 
specification and high raw material costs. We expect 4Q26E revenues to decline by 
4% QoQ, considering the high base in 3Q26 and the gradual ramp up of new AI 
server racks. As we highlighted in Oct (Global Server TAM updates), we expect global 
AI server racks (powered by NVIDIA and AMD) shipment at 63k / 110k / 164k in 
2026E-28E along with the specification upgrade to drive the ASP; we raised leading 
US CSPs in aggregate capex by 4% / 17% / 22% in 2026E-28E to US$1,397bn by 
2028E, reflecting our positive view on AI spending. Maintain Neutral on fair 
valuation.

Earnings revision: We raise revenues by 1% / 23% / 30% in 2026E-28E to reflect the 
AI server racks shipment ramp up and higher ASP on better specification. Given the

much lower GM of AI server racks compared to Quanta’s other products, our changes in 
gross profits narrow to 2% / 5% / 5% in 2026E-28E. Our opex ratio remains largely 
unchanged in 2026E-27E, while further improves in 2028E reflecting the enhancing 
operation efficiency when revenues scale increases and harvest from previous R&D 
investments. We change our non-op income and tax rate estimates in 2026E-28E closer 
to historical trend. Our net income is thus changed by -2% / +0% / +14% in 2026E-28E.

Valuation: We continue to use a near-term P/E to derive our 12M TP . Our target P/E 
multiple is at 10.7x 2027E (no change), which is derived from the refreshed peers’ 
correlation between forward trading P/E and earnings growth and Quanta’s forward year 
earnings growth. With our updated earnings estimates our 12M TP is revised up to 
NT$300 (vs. NT$299 previously). Our target P/E is still between the company’s historical 
avg. and avg. -1stv. trading P/E. Maintain Neutral.

### Page 3

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Quanta 
We are Neutral rated on Quanta. Our 12-month target price of NT$300 is based on an 
10.7x 2027E P/E, which references PC/server peers’ EPS growth vs. P/E multiple 
correlation. We see EPS growth as a major factor for the stock’s performance. Key 
upside/downside risks include: 1) stronger-/weaker-than-expected PC market 
recovery; 2) faster-/slower-than-expected AI server ramp-up; and 3) 
stronger-/weaker-than-expected demand for general servers.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

水冷機櫃與機殼本業同步走升 
 2Q26 本業穩健成長 ，EPS 1.41 元：晟銘電 2Q26 營收 32.19 億元，
年增 22.5%，毛利率則從前期 21.1%降至 20.5%，主要是因客戶
Storage 相關產品出貨增加，然 Storage 產品已進入成熟階段，客戶
持續每季議價，2Q26 稅後淨利為 2.9 億元，EPS 1.41 元。 
 預期 3Q26 為營運谷底，4Q26 開始爬升：展望 26H2，3Q26 營收年
增 16%，估計 3Q26 稅後 EPS 1.49 元。4Q 通常為傳統旺季，加上
VR200 也會在 4Q26 陸續加入量產，而晟銘電也有參與其中，出貨

系統整合業務營收將進一步提升。

殼與 ASIC 放量，2027 年營運可望進一步成長，且公司已成功切入
Meta 供應鏈，並在冷卻機櫃與機構件取得關鍵認證，可直接承接
Meta 釋出的巨額硬體訂單，且在液冷架構成為主流帶動下，晟銘電
水冷相關業務比重持續提升 ， 使2026 年整體毛利率有望升到 20%以
上 ， 投顧預估2026 年 EPS 6.13 元，2027 年 EPS 9.4 元 ， 維持買進 ，
目標價 140 元。

晟銘電 為伺服器機殼大廠，目前
切入 IT 機櫃、水冷機櫃、水冷搭
配的分歧管 
 晟銘電子科技股份有限公司成立於 1976 年 6 月 ， 為台灣伺服器機殼OEM/ODM
製造商， 公司主要銷售電腦機殼及伺服器機殼 ，其用途為桌上型電腦及伺服器等
各項組件之安裝防護設備 ， 近年來公司積極跨足散熱領域並研發設計出液冷散熱
的浸沒式散熱產品。於 2024 年公司推出多款液冷機櫃及液冷散熱系統。晟銘電
提供全球客戶迅速且彈性的 OEM 及 ODM 生產服務。2026 年 1~8 月主要產品
營收占比，伺服器 73%、L11 系統整合 18%、Tooling 6%、DT 3%。

Exhibit 3: 產品組合

Sources : Company、MasterLink

2Q26 本業穩健成長，EPS 1.41
元 
 晟銘電 2Q26 營收 32.19 億元，年增 22.5%，毛利率則從前期 21.1%降至
20.5%，主要是因客戶 Storage 相關產品出貨增加，然 Storage 產品已進入成熟
階段，客戶持續每季議價，2Q26 稅後淨利為 2.9 億元，EPS 1.41 元。

預期 3Q26 為營運谷底，4Q26
開始爬升 
 展望 26H2，3Q26 營收年增 16%，估計 3Q26 稅後 EPS 1.49 元。4Q 通常為
傳統旺季，加上 VR200 也會在 4Q26 陸續加入量產，而晟銘電也有參與其中，
出貨量將逐步上升，隨著 VR200 及 ASIC 等新平台陸續進入放量階段，預期
機殼與 Sidecar 出貨量將同步提升。此外，2026 年 ODM 專案有 8 至 9 成聚焦
機櫃業務，涵蓋 OCP、EIA 以及 MGX，預期 L11 系統整合業務營收將進一步
提升。

今年前八月 L11 系統組裝營收占比已達 18％ ， 而明年L11 系統組裝營收貢獻還
會持續提升。公司在機櫃產品線已相當完整，還可提供配套的水冷配件，機箱
基本上是跟隨著輝達的腳步走 ， 包括GB300、VR200 等標準品機箱都已經開好
模具。海外產能布局，公司目前生產基地仍以中國大陸為主，其中東莞為主要
生產基地，另於寧波設有產能，產品以機殼、機櫃為主，東莞廠自動烤漆線預
計今年底前完成，一天可以完成60 個機櫃烤漆；台灣中壢則主要負責打樣及水
冷產品研發，部分零組件仍由中國生產後運送至中壢進行後段組裝。泰國一期
廠已完成建置並具備生產條件 ， 二期則持續進行設備調校 ， 主要導入烤漆產線，
預計 4Q26 開始試產，後續待客戶完成稽核及認證後逐步量產。泰國二期的烤

漆設備規格除可支援機殼外，亦可延伸至機櫃產品，有助未來將部分機櫃製程
移往海外。今年中壢廠、泰國廠都有新產能加入，而明年資本支出大致也會維
持在大約 7～8 億元。

維持買進，目標價 140 元 
 投顧認為晟銘電 4Q26 起受惠 VR200 機殼與 ASIC 放量，2027 年營運可望進
一步成長 ， 且公司已成功切入Meta 供應鏈 ， 並在冷卻機櫃與機構件取得關鍵認
證，可直接承接Meta 釋出的巨額硬體訂單，且在液冷架構成為主流帶動下，晟
銘電水冷相關業務比重持續提升，使 2026 年整體毛利率有望升到 20%以上，
投顧預估 2026 年 EPS 6. 13 元，2027 年 EPS 9.4 元，維持買進，目標價 140
元。(15x2027EPS)

風險提示

1) 客戶機箱導入狀況。 
2) L11 系統組裝發展狀況。

(3) 預估 2026 年 EPS 2 4.93 元、 2027 年 EPS 42. 45 元 ， 投 資 建 議
買 進 ， 目 標 價 1,6 00 元 。

2 群益投顧 
  ESG ：  
因 應 企 業 永 續 經 營 所 帶 來 的 挑 戰 與 機 會，整 合 各 方 利 害 關 係 人 關 注 之 議 題，
將 展 開 「 永 續 經 營(ESG) 計 畫 」 ， 成 立 企 業 永 續 發 展 委 員 會 ， 聚 焦 於 五 大 策
略 ， 連 結 聯 合 國 「 永 續 發 展 目 標 SDGs 」 ， 以 企 業 核 心 精 神 與 專 業 ， 落 實 永
續 經 營 各 項 目 標，促 進 永 續 的 經 濟 成 長、社 會 發 展 與 環 境 保 護，提 升 企 業 競
爭 力 與 發 揮 影 響 力 。 
公 司 簡 介 ： 
藥華藥主要致力於研發和生產新一代創新型生物藥，特別聚焦於血液腫瘤
領 域 。 公 司 旗 艦 產 品 為 Ropeginterferon alfa -2b ( 簡稱 Ropeg ，商品名
Besremi) ， 是 一 個 創 新 的 長 效 型 干 擾 素 藥 物 ， 目 前 已 在 美 國 、 歐 洲 、 日 本 、
韓 國 、 台 灣 及 中 國 等 全 球 約 50 個 國 家 取 得 藥 證 ， 用 於 治 療 成 人 真 性 紅 血 球
增多症 (Polycythemia Vera, PV) ， 並 已 陸 續 於 台 灣 、 日 本 及 美 國 取 得 原 發 性
血 小 板 過 多 症(ET) 藥 證 。  
Ropeg 是 藥 華 藥 自 行 研 發、並 擁 有 全 球 獨 家 權 利 的 新 藥。公 司 利 用 其 特 有 的
長 效 型 蛋 白 質 藥 物 技 術 平 台 ， 成 功 開 發 出Ropeg 。 過 去 PV 的 治 療 藥 物 有 限
且 常 伴 隨 副 作 用 ， 包 含 老 藥 HU 及 尚 未 獲 PV 適 應 症 批 准 的 干 擾 素 藥 物 ， 與
美 國 藥 廠 Incyte 開 發 的 Jakafi (ruxolitinib) 。 2019 年， Ropeg 獲 得 歐 洲 藥 品
管理局 (EMA) 核 准 上 市 ， 並 在 2021 年 11 月 獲 得 美 國 FDA 的 核 准 ， 成 為 第
一個被 FDA 批 准 用 於 所 有 PV 成 人 的 治 療 藥 物 ， 不 受 之 前 治 療 史 的 限 制 ，
也 是 第 一 個 被 FDA 核 准 特 別 用 於 PV 的 干 擾 素 療 法 ， 且 已 連 續 四 年 獲NCCN
治 療 指 引 推 薦 。 
相 較 於 傳 統 干 擾 素 ，Ropeg 具 有 更 長 的 半 衰 期 ， 因 此 可 以 延 長 給 藥 間 隔 ， 提
高 病 患 的 便 利 性 及 順 從 性，同 時 也 展 現 出 更 優 異 的 療 效。 在 7.3 年 的 臨 床 追
蹤 數 據 顯 示 76% 的 完 全 血 液 學 反 應 率(CHR) ， 並 能 持 續 降 低 JAK2 基 因 突 變
負 擔 。 藥 華 藥 在 美 國 、 歐 洲 、 日 本 及 台 灣 等 地 設 有 子 公 司 ， 負 責 藥 物 的 銷
售 與 推 廣 。Ropeg 目 前 是 公 司 最 主 要 的 營 收 來 源 ， 佔 比 超 過9 成 。 此 外 也 積
極拓展 Ropeg 的 其 他 適 應 症 ， 包 含 血 小 板 過 多 症(ET) 及 骨 髓 纖 維 化(MF) 等。。 
3Q26 營 收 季 增，9 月 未 含 CMS 調 整 仍 創 新 高，財 務 營 運 槓 桿 與 獲 利 結 構 持
續 優 化 ：  
2Q2 6 營收 68. 55 億元 (YoY+90.30%) ， 主 要 源 於 美 國 與 日 本 兩 大 高 價 值 市 場
銷 量 顯 著 成 長 ， 團 隊 擴 編 與 精 準 醫 學 行 銷 策 略 效 益 大 舉 顯 現 。 毛 利 率
94.2 3%， 受 惠 美 國 5M2 6 調 升 藥 價，以及 CMS 回沖， 營 業 費 用 33.00 億元
(YoY +60.68%) ， 增 加 主 因 延 續 團 隊 擴 編 、 商 業 推 廣 以 及 依 會 計 準 則 認 列 限
制 員 工 權 利 新 股 約 2 億多元 ，增 幅 遠 低 於 營 收 成 長，展 現 高 營 運 槓 桿 之 強 勁
爆發力 。  
ET 新 適 應 症 在 全 球 步 入 收 割 期 ， 併 購 予 宇 切 入 新 興 療 法 ， 加 上PMF 收案
順 利 與 肝 癌 術 後 100% 防 復 發 數 據 ， 中 長 期 佈 局 完 整：  
原 發 性 血 小 板 過 多 症(ET) 為過去 30 年 來 缺 乏 新 藥 之 領 域 ， 公 司 於05/12/2026
率 先 取 得 台 灣 ET 藥 證 ， 用 於 HU 抗 藥 性 或 無 法 耐 受 之 成 人 ET 病 人 ； 日 本
MHLW 專 家 會 議 於 07/2026 通 過 審 查 ， 破 例 同 意 將 全 球 三 期 SURPASS -ET
與 北 美 單 臂 二 期 XC 數 據 合 併 評 估 ， 並 已 於 08/24/2026 正 式 獲 日 本 核 准 用

於 所 有 成 人 ET 患 者 ， 包 括 未 曾 接 受 治 療 者 ； 隨 後 美 國FDA 亦 正 式 核 准 Ropeg
用 於 所 有 成 人 ET 患 者 ， 成 為 近 30 年首款 ET 新 藥 ， 並 具 7 年 孤 兒 藥 獨 佔
期，配 合 芝 加 哥 誓 師 大 會 已 完 成 之 團 隊 培 訓 與 推 廣 文 宣，取 證 後 已 立 即 展 開
商 業 推 廣 ； 其 他 市 場 方 面 ， 中 國 大 陸 與 韓 國ET 審 查 同 步 進 行 ， 分 別 預 計 於

請。早 期 原 發 性 骨 髓 纖 維 化(PMF) 三 期 臨 床 HOPE -PMF 於 06/2026 完 成 收 案
且 超 收 至 160 人 (原目標 150 人 )， 預 計 2027 年 完 成 試 驗 、2028 年 中 申 請 藥
證。  
在 實 體 腫 瘤 領 域 ，B 型 肝 炎 相 關 肝 癌(HCC) 術 後 防 復 發 一 期 試 驗 ，Ropeg 序
列合併 Anti -PD1 在 15 名 患 者 中 位 追 蹤 1,024 天 ， 創 下 100% 無 復 發 存 活 率
(RFS) 之 重 大 突 破 。PEG -IL -2 (P11838) 、 PD -1-IL -2v 、 ADC 及 KRAS TCR -T
將於 2026 -2027 年 陸 續 申 請 IND 。  
藥 華 藥 近 期 斥 資 約 1.68 億 元 收 購 予 宇 生 技 100% 股 權 ， 取 得 脂 質 奈 米 顆 粒
(LNP) 藥 物 遞 送 平 台 、 研 發 設 備 及 專 業 團 隊 ， 並 納 入 兩 項 新 藥 研 發 專 案 。 予
宇成立於 2019 年， LNP 平 台 涵 蓋 配 方 設 計 、 微 流 體 製 程 與 分 析 方 法 ， 可 支
援 mRNA 、 saRNA 及 circRNA 等 多 元 RNA 藥 物 開 發 ； 原 有 FR -202 與 FR -
201 兩 項 專 案 分 別 鎖 定 端 粒 生 物 學 疾 病 與 威 爾 森 氏 症 ， 預 計 於 2027 年及
2028 年 提 出 IND 。  
此 項 收 購 對 公 司 營 運 具 三 大 正 面 意 義 ：(1) 收 購 金 額 僅 約 1.68 億 元 ， 相 對 公
司單季逾 25 億 元 淨 利 規 模 ， 財 務 負 擔 極 低 ；(2) 取 得 遞 送 系 統 自 主 能 力 ， 可
與公司既有蛋白質藥物製造資源整合，從研發早期即同步規劃藥物設計與
後 續 製 造 ；(3) 延 伸 研 發 版 圖 至 In Vivo CAR -T 此 一 國 際 大 廠 積 極 布 局 的 領
域 ， 提 升 中 長 期 選 擇 權 價 值 ， 亦 為 後 續 研 發 產 品 線 布 局 增 加 深 度 與 廣 度 。 
注 射 筆 上 市 與 潛 力 患 者 數 上 修 雙 輪 驅 動，配 合 真 實 世 界 數 據 防止 惡 化，鞏 固
Ropeg 在 PV 領 域 之 地 位 ：  
Ropeg 在 PV 適 應 症 已 於 全 球 約 50 個 國 家 獲 批 ， 加 拿 大 亦 於 07/02/2026 
順 利 取 證 。 美 國 FDA 於 06/2026 核准 Ropeg 注 射 筆 ， 並 於 07/2026 正 式 出
貨 開 賣，注 射 筆 具 備 極 細 針 頭、精 準 調 劑 及 一 次 性 自 動 縮 回 防 針 扎 設 計，短
期 可 加 速 替 換 傳 統 預 充 充 填 針 筒 Prefilled Syringe ， 中 長 期 將 提 升 患 者 長 期
用 藥 依 從 性 與 留 存 率 。 
武田製藥 (Takeda) 競品 Rusfertide (擬 似 鐵 調 素)預 計 於 08/2026 獲 批，藥 華 藥
指出  Rusfertide 僅 定 位 於 配 合 放 血 之 被 動 症 狀 控 制 ， 而 Ropeg 為 一 線 首 選
積 極 療 法 ， 真 實 世 界 數 據 證 實 Ropeg 合 併 治 療 可 使 患 者 疾 病 惡 化成 骨 髓 纖
維化  (MF )的 概 率 維 持 在 0%。最 新 流 行 病 學 文 獻 將 美 國 MPN 總 潛 力 患 者 數
上修至 34.70 萬人 ( 原 30.50 萬人 )， 競 品 進 入市 場 將 大 幅 提 升 早 期 治 療 之 病
識 感 與 診 斷 率 ， 反 而 有 助 於 Ropeg 進 一 步 擴 大 一 線 市 場 壟 斷 地 位 。 
藥 華 藥 與 AOP 仲 裁 案 ： 
關於 AOP 第 二 階 段 仲 裁 判 斷 ， 藥 華 藥 認 為 其 程 序 存 在 違 反 程 序 正 義 及 聽 審
權 之 重 大 瑕 疵 ， 目 前 正 與 外 部 律 師 團 隊 嚴 謹 評 估 ， 將 在 法 定 時 限 內 向 德 國
法 蘭 克 福 高 等 法 院 提 起 「 撤 銷 第 二 階 段 仲 裁 判 斷 之 訴 」 。 
由 於 法 律 程 序 仍 在 評 估 中 ， 現 階 段 無 法 判 斷 或 預 測 該 裁 定 金 額 發 生 的 時 間
點 ， 後 續 發 展 視 訴 訟 進 度 而 定 。 公 司 與AOP 之 合 約 目 前 仍 處 於 有 效 狀 態 ，

4 群益投顧 
對 於 任 何 有 助 於 妥 善 解 決 爭 議 、 維 護 股 東 權 益 之 方 案 均 持 開 放 態 度 。 歐 洲
仍 為 藥 華 藥 極 為 重 視 之 核 心 市 場 ， 將 綜 合 評 估 法 規 要 求 、 商 業 推 廣 與 契 約
關 係 ， 持 續 規 劃 並 評 估 於 歐 洲 申 請 ET 藥 證 之 可 行 性 。 
AOP 在 8/7/2026 發 布 的 官 方 新 聞 稿 表 示 ， 藥 華 藥 延 遲 造 成 的 損 害 約€82M ，
2019 -2022 過 高 價 格 所 產 生 的 退 款 約 €31M ， AOP 原 本 應 支 付 給 藥 華 藥 的
profit sharing 約 17M 可 用 來 抵 銷 ； 最 終 AOP 表 示 自 己 不 用 付 款 給 藥 華 藥 ，
並 認 為 藥 華 藥 目 前 對 AOP 的 責 任 已 進 入 「 三 位 數 百 萬 歐 元 」 範 圍 。 推 論 藥
華 藥 實 際 曝 險 金 額 Net Set -off 後 約 為 €97.48M (約新台幣 36 億元 )。  
AOP 提 出 延 伸 主 張 ， 若 將 仲 裁 庭 對 2019 -2022 年 價 格 爭 議( 最高 900% 超額
定 價 之 認 定)的 計 算 邏 輯 套 用 至 2023 -2025 年供貨， AOP 主 張 藥 華 藥 需 再 退
還約 €65M ， 此 項 目 僅 為 AOP 單 方 面 之 延 伸 主 張 ， 非 現 階 段 確 定 之 仲 裁 判
決。  
藥 華 藥 已 從 純 研 發 型 生 技 公 司 轉 型 為 具 備 強 勁 商 業 化 現 金 流 之 藥 廠 ， 一 次
性認列約 36 億 元 損 失 衝 擊 ， 不 至 於 構 成 營 運 生 存 危 機。

投 資 建 議：(1)07/2026 於 美 國 開 賣 Ropeg 注 射 筆 提 升 患 者 用 藥 順 應 性 與 留 存
率 ， 推 升 低 風 險 PV/ET 患 者 滲 透 率 ， 為 2H26 營 收 加 速 引 擎 ；(2)ET 新 適 應
症 迎 來 多 國 藥 證 收 割，開 啟 第 二 成 長 曲 線，台 灣、日本 取 證 後，美國 FDA 亦
核 准 用 於 所 有 成 人 ET 患 者 並 具 7 年 孤 兒 藥 獨 佔 期，中 國 與 韓 國 分 別 預 計 於
11/2026 與 12/2026 取 證 ， 並 準 備 於 澳 洲 啟 動 PV 及 ET 雙 適 應 症 藥 證 申 請。
(3) 預估 2026 年 EPS 2 4.93 元、 2027 年 EPS 42. 45 元 ， 投 資 建 議 買 進 ， 目 標
價 1,6 00 元 。

7 群益投顧 
  【 投 資 評 等 說 明 】 
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy)  5%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<15 %

中立 (Neutral)  
無 法 由 基 本 面 給 予 投 資 評 等 
預 期 近 期 股 價 將 處 於 盤 整 
建 議 降 低 持 股

【 投 資 評 等 調 整 機 制 】 
  投 資 評 等 係「 首 次 」給 予 特 定 個 股 投 資 評 等 時，其 前 一 日 收 盤 價 相 較3 個月
目 標 價 之 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 計 算 而 得 。 個 股 投 資 評 等 分 為四 個 等 級 ， 定 義 如 上 。
爾 後 的 投 資 評 等係 依 循 「 首 次 評 等 」 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。  
「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」及 「 區 間 操 作 」均有 upside 目 標 價 。差 別 僅 在 於 ，
首 次 評 等 時 潛 在 報 酬 率 不 同 。 「 中 立 」 則 無 目 標 價 。 
一 旦 我 們 給 予 特 定 個 股「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」或「 區 間 操 作 」之 投 資 評 等，
就 是 責 任 的 開 始，爾 後 將 透 過 各 式 研 究 報 告 作 定 期 性、持 續 性 追 蹤 基 本 面 及
股 價 變 化 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。 
停 止 推 薦 情 境 ： 
1.  達 目 標 價 。 
2.  雖 未 達 原 訂 目 標 價 ， 但 檢 視 基 本 面 、 訊 息 面 、 籌 碼 面 等 多 方 訊 息 ， 研
判 股 價 上 漲 空 間 已 然 有 限 ， 將 適 時 出 具 降 評 報 告 。 
3.  推 薦 後 股 價 不 漲 反 跌 ， 亦 將 出 具 降 評 報 告 。

【免責聲明】  
  本 研 究 報 告 僅 提 供 予 特 定 人 之 客 戶 作 為 參 考 資 料「 非 經 同 意 不 得 轉 載 」 。我
們 並 不 確 保 此 資 訊 的 完 整 性 與 正 確 性，投 資 人 應 了 解，報 告 中 有 關 未 來 預 測
之 陳 述 可 能 不 會 實 現，因 而 不 應 被 依 賴。而 且 此 報 告 並 非 根 據 特 定 投 資 目 的
或 依 預 定 對 象 之 財 務 狀 況 所 撰 寫 出 來 的，因 此，此 研 究 報 告 的 目 的，既 非 對
投 資 人 於 買 賣 證 券、選 擇 權、期 貨 或 其 他 證 券 相 關 之 衍 生 性 商 品 提 供 詢 價 服
務，亦 非 作 為 進 行 交 易 的 要 約。投 資 人 應 注 意 到 相 關 證 券 之 價 值 及 收 益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

個股聚焦

陳奕均 fion.chen@sinopac.com

泰合 (6467 TT) 
開花結果在即

永豐觀點 
抗血栓口溶膜貼片 BEQIA 歐美取證在即。 
 投資評價與建議 
投資建議買進：泰合 10/20 上市掛牌，掛牌價105.6 元。由於(1) 泰合開發之口溶
膜劑型藥物有效解決高齡與吞嚥困難之臨床痛點下，市場競爭力足；(2) 抗血栓口
溶膜貼片 BEQIA 歐美取證在即，2027 年在授權與上市權利金開始認列下 ，營運轉
虧為盈且呈大跳躍狀；(3) 由於多數學名藥廠受限專利訴訟，最快需至 2028 年 4
月後才可上市銷售，促使泰合享有約一年的「黃金市場空窗期」 ，能與原廠直接瓜
分市場。且因 BEQIA 屬 505(b)(2)新劑型新藥，即便2Q28 後學名藥陸續進場，其
獨特的劑型優勢仍能築起價格防護牆，所面臨的價折壓力相對輕微，建議買進，目
標價 170 元( 18 X 2027 EPS )。

營運現況與分析 
公司簡介：泰合生技成立於 2010 年，專注開發 505(b)(2)新劑型新藥與特殊學名
藥，核心技術為自主研發的 TDS 透皮／透黏膜傳輸平台，將現有口服藥品成分改
造為透皮與透黏膜藥物傳導劑型 ，提供方便使用且更安全的新劑型藥物。 產品開發
上，505(b)(2)部分，治療抗血栓口溶膜貼片(每日兩片)BEQIA 靜待歐美取證中外，
其餘則仍在臨床階段中，困難學名藥部分，化療止吐口溶膜2018 年日本取證銷售
中外，其餘四項藥品則在進行洽談授權合作。 
圖一：泰合產品線開發時程

資料來源：泰合；永豐投顧研究部整理，Oct. 2026

### Page 2

2

口溶膜劑型藥物能有效解決高齡與吞嚥困難之臨床痛點 ：傳統口服錠劑藥物必須經
過食道、進入胃腸道消化，再經由肝臟代謝後才進入血液循環，泰合開發之口溶膜
劑型藥物， 可讓藥膜在接觸舌頭或口腔黏膜數秒內快速溶解 ， 病患完全不需飲水即
可完成給藥， 且部份藥物分子能直接透過口腔黏膜上皮細胞微血管吸收， 直接進入
全身血液循環， 不僅能提高藥物生體可用率， 還能減少胃酸對藥物的破壞與對胃腸
道的刺激下，有效解決高齡與吞嚥困難之臨床痛點，提高患者服藥順從性。 
抗血栓口溶膜貼片 BEQIA 歐美取證在即，提前卡位下，營運動能可期：泰合自主
開發的抗血栓口溶膜新劑型新藥 BEQIA， 預計4Q26 取得歐盟藥證 、2Q27 獲美國
FDA 核准上市。目前該藥原廠在美國市場年銷售額>200 億美元，相較原廠口服錠
劑，泰合 BEQIA 具備三大競爭優勢：不需飲水（服藥便利）、解決吞嚥困難（減
輕照顧端壓力），以及攜帶與給藥便利（優化長照與醫療機構給藥流程）。 
市場競爭方面，由於多數學名藥廠受限專利訴訟，最快需至2028 年 4 月後才可上
市銷售，促使泰合享有約一年的「黃金市場空窗期」 ，能與原廠直接瓜分市場。此
外，由於BEQIA 屬 505(b)(2)新劑型新藥，即便2Q28 後學名藥陸續進場，其獨特
的劑型優勢仍能築起價格防護牆，所面臨的折價壓力應相對輕微。 
泰合海外市場營銷預計將採授權分潤模式，研究部預估 1H27 歐美地區簽約金應
有機會各達 500~1000 萬美金外，隨美國地區 2Q27 上市、通路鋪設及醫保陸續
准入，Peak sales 市佔率 2-3%(預期將落於 2029-30 年)、銷售分潤 12-15%估算
下，稅後 EPS 貢獻將落於 10-20 元。 
財務預估：預估泰合 2026 年營收 0.1 億元，稅後虧損 1.89 億元，稅後 EPS 以掛
牌後股本 5.99 億元估算，稅後 EPS-3.15 元，2027 年預估歐美地區授權簽約金各
500 萬美金及 1000 萬美金 ， 及上市銷售後權利金認列下 ，預估營收將跳升至7.75
億元，稅後淨利 5.68 億元，稅後 EPS 9.49 元。

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 긴 호흡으로삼성전자(005930) 긴 호흡으로긴 호흡으로 - 기업.pdf

### Page 1

(005930) 
삼성전자 
2026.09.29

반도체 손인준 
6154 / sij@eugenefn.com

긴 호흡으로 
 투자의견 STRONG BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 560,000원(유지) 
 현재주가 270,000원(9/28)

 삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익이 103.6조원(+16%qoq)을 기록할 것으로 전
망한다. 메모리 가격 등 전반적인 반도체 업황에 대한 기존 가정은 대체로 
유지했으나, 최근 원화 강세에 따라 원/달러 환율 가정을 1,440원에서 
1,400원으로 낮추고 MX 사업부의 적자 확대를 반영해 실적 추정치를 하
향 조정했다. 또한 2Q26 재고가 3Q26 매출로 인식되는 과정에서 발생하
는 추가 성과급 충당금 부담도 반영했다.  
 DS 부문의 3Q26 영업이익은 104.0조원(+17%qoq)을 기록할 것으로 전
망한다. 서버 DRAM과 eSSD 중심의 가격 인상이 이어지며, DRAM과 
NAND의 Blended ASP는 각각 +17%, +18%QoQ 상승할 것으로 추정한
다. 1cnm 수율 개선과 HBM4의 빠른 램프업도 실적 개선에 기여할 전망이
다. Foundry/LSI는 HBM4 베이스다이 생산 확대, Groq 3 LPU 양산 및 선
단 공정 가격 인상에 힘입어 하반기 매출 성장이 예상된다. 다만 성과급 충
당금과 감가상각비 부담으로 영업적자 축소 폭은 제한적일 것으로 보인다. 
 DX 부문의 3Q26 영업이익은 -1.8조원(적자지속qoq)을 기록할 것으로 추
정한다. 삼성전자는 메모리 가격 급등에도 제품 가격 인상을 제한하며 시장 
점유율 확대에 주력하고 있다. 이러한 가격 정책과 갤럭시 Z 폴드8 등 신제
품 흥행에 힘입어 판매량은 당초 예상치를 상회할 것으로 보인다. 다만 원
가 상승분의 전가가 제한되면서 영업적자 폭은 크게 확대될 전망이다.  
 삼성전자의 4Q26 영업이익은 114.9조원(+11%qoq)을 기록할 것으로 전
망한다. DRAM과 NAND의 Blended ASP는 각각 +11%, +12%QoQ 상승
하며 견조한 흐름을 이어갈 것으로 보인다. 환율 가정 조정에 따라 2026년
과 2027년 영업이익 추정치를 각각 365.2조원(+735%yoy), 595.8조원
(+63%YoY)으로 낮췄다. 4Q26~1Q27 범용 DRAM·NAND 및 2027년 
HBM 가격 협상 환경은 공급업체에 유리하게 전개되고 있어, 향후 가격 가
정의 추가 상향 가능성이 높다고 판단한다.  
 극심한 DRAM 공급 부족으로 인한 HBM 적층 단수 하향 조정(base die 수
요 증가), Memory Wall에 대응하기 위한 3D Stacked DRAM·zHBM 도입 
논의 등은 파운드리의 기술적 가치를 부각시키는 요인이다. 파운드리의 수
익성 회복 속도는 완만할 전망이지만, 메모리와 파운드리를 연계한 턴키 비
즈니스의 시너지는 AI 반도체 시장 내 경쟁력을 높이고, 메모리 시장 점유
율 확대에도 기여할 것으로 보인다. 적극적인 메모리 가격 인상 정책으로 
업계 최고 수준의 Blended ASP도 장기간 유지할 것으로 예상한다. 
 이제 메모리 업종 주가는 업황의 지속 가능성을 확인하고 꾸준한 주주환원
을 반영하며, 중장기적으로 점진적인 상승세를 이어갈 것으로 판단한다. 삼
성전자는 메모리·파운드리를 아우르는 턴키 역량과 가격 정책에 기반한 실
적 차별화를 통해 피어 그룹 내 경쟁력을 높여갈 것으로 보인다.

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

Document 178: [207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 신규 공장이 채운 실적, 수주가 더할 모멘텀삼성바이오로직스(207940) 신규 공장이 채운 실적, 수주가 더_21961458

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 신규 공장이 채운 실적, 수주가 더할 모멘텀삼성바이오로직스(207940) 신규 공장이 채운 실적, 수주가 더_21961458.pdf

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
미국 록빌·5공장 가세로 3분기 기대치 부합 전망 
삼성바이오로직스의 2026년 3분기 연결 매출액은 1조 3,637억 원(+8.4% Y oY), 
영업이익은 6,092억 원(-3.8% YoY, 영업이익률 44.7%)으로 시장 기대치에 부합할 
전망이다. 5월 파업에 따른 생산 차질에도 1~4공장(총 60.4만 리터)의 풀가동과 
신규 공장의 매출 기여가 외형 성장을 뒷받침할 것으로 예상한다. 미국 록빌 
공장(6만 리터)은 3분기부터 매출 기여가 본격화되고, 5공장(18만 리터)도 
인증용(PPQ) 배치 생산을 통해 매출이 확대될 전망이다. 2분기에는 미국 공장·5공장 
관련 비용이 선반영됐으나, 3분기에는 매출 증가에 따른 고정비 부담 완화로 
영업이익률이 전분기 대비 0.3%p 상승할 것으로 예상한다. 
항체 중심 CDMO에서 펩타이드로 확장 
삼성바이오로직스의 2026년 신규 수주는 약 1.2조 원으로 전년 수주액의 약 20% 
수준에 그치고 있으나, 연말부터 내년까지 수주 모멘텀은 점차 강화될 것으로 
판단한다. 2025년 하반기 이후 미국의 의약품 관세 불확실성이 완화되며 글로벌 
제약사와의 논의가 늘어나고 있어, 신규 수주 계약으로 이어질 것으로 기대된다. 
11월 유상증자 납입이 마무리되면 6공장(18만 리터) 착공과 스위스 폴리펩타이드 
인수가 본격화되며 중장기 성장 기대감도 높아질 전망이다. 특히 폴리펩타이드 
인수가 연내 완료되면 2027년부터 연결 실적에 반영되는 동시에, 항체 의약품 
중심의 CDMO 사업을 GLP-1 등 펩타이드로 확장하는 계기가 될 전망이다. 
목표주가 200만 원으로 하향, 수주 회복이 관건 
삼성바이오로직스의 목표주가를 209만 원에서 200만 원으로 하향한다. 유상증자 
신주 발행에 따른 주당가치 4.7% 희석 효과를 반영했다. 폴리펩타이드 인수 효과는 
거래완료 이후 실적 추정치에 반영할 예정이다. 노조 파업 불확실성이 주가에 
부담으로 작용할 수 있어 신규 수주 모멘텀의 회복이 필요한 시점이다. 
목표주가 (하향) 2,000,000원  
현재가 (10/6) 1,310,000원

그림 1. 삼성바이오로직스 수주 총액

자료: DART, IBK투자증권 
주: 2026년 수주 총액은 2026년 반기 보고서 기준($21,689M에 신규 수주 $331M 합산)

Document 194: AIと金融市場、強まる相互依存 ～IMFの国際金融安定性報告書から読み解く新たなリスク～

Source PDF: all report pdf/AIと金融市場、強まる相互依存 ～IMFの国際金融安定性報告書から読み解く新たなリスク～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

Flash Insights

2026 年10 月 15 日

1/5 
AI と金融市場、強まる相互依存 
～IMF の国際金融安定性報告書から読み解く新たなリスク～ 
 資産運用調査部 主席研究員  奥田 宏二

1. はじめに 
国際通貨基金（IMF）は10 月 13 日、国際金融安定性報告書（Global Financial Stability 
Report、GFSR）の全体版を公表した。金融市場は底堅さを維持していると評価したうえで、金融
安定上のリスクは高いとした。主なリスクとして①人工知能（AI）関連資産の大幅な値下がり、
②国債利回りの急上昇、③サイバー攻撃の三つを取り上げた。 
GFSRは世界経済の動向を分析する「世界経済見通し」 （WEO）と並ぶIMFの主要な定期報告書だ。
株式・債券市場や銀行、ノンバンクなどを幅広く点検し、金融システムの安定を損なうリスクや
ショックを増幅させる脆弱性を分析する。各国の金融当局や市場関係者にとって、その時々の金
融市場・システムの課題を示すものとして注目度が高い。本稿では、GFSRが今回示した三つのリ
スクを整理する（資料1）。特にAIが金融市場・システムにもたらす影響を詳しくみていく。

資料 1 GFSR（2026 年 10 月版）があげた三つのリスク

（出所）IMF「GFSR（2026 年 10 月）」より第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成 
リスク 評価の変化 評価の概要
AI関連資産
の大幅な値
下がり
横ばい
引き金：AI関連投資の規模や持続性、収益性が期待を下回る。
評価：AI関連資産の規模が拡大し ているため、影響は大きくな
り得る。中核的なAI関連企業の収益は好調で、株価の割高感は
和らいだ一方、AI関連分野でレバレッジが拡大し ている。
国債利回り
の急上昇
悪化
引き金：インフレ再燃、財政問題でタームプレミア ムが上昇。
評価：国債市場は金融市場の基盤で影響は深刻になり得る。高
水準の政府債務や借り換えリスクは未解決のため、財政・イン
フレのショックをより頻繁に受けやすい。
サイバー攻
撃
悪化
引き金：重要な金融機関や市場インフラがサイバー攻撃で被害
評価：デジタル化の急速な進展で攻撃対象が広がり、影響は深
刻になり得る。最先端のAIモデルは攻撃と防御の双方に利用さ
れる可能性。

### Page 2

2/5 
2. AI関連資産の大幅な値下がりリスク  
一つ目のリスクにあげたのはAI関連資産の大幅な値下がりだ。4 月版からの変化を「横ばい」と
評価した。しかし、これは状況が変わっていないという意味ではない。AI関連の中核企業が高い
利益成長を続け、株価の割高感が薄れた点を評価した一方、巨額の設備投資によるフリーキャッ
シュフローの減少や社債発行の増加、投資家によるレバレッジ拡大を警戒している。 
IMFは、AI関連投資を世界経済や金融市場の重要な下支え要因と評価している。生成AIなどの普
及に伴い、データセンターや半導体投資は活発で、関連企業の業績は好調だ。AI関連企業の株価
が急上昇した一方、予想利益も大きく伸びている。GFSRは、一部の株式市場で予想利益の伸びが
株価上昇を上回り、予想株価収益率（PER）が低下した点を評価している。 
一方、リスクが高まっている点として注目するのは、大規模なクラウドサービスやデータセン
ターを運営する企業（ハイパースケーラー）のフリーキャッシュフロー（事業で稼いだ現金から
設備投資などを差し引いた額）の減少だ。設備投資の急増により、資金調達のため社債発行が拡
大している。IMFによると、世界の投資適格社債発行額に占めるハイパースケーラーの割合は、
2024 年の1％未満から2026 年には約10％に上昇した（資料2）。社債の信用スプレッドも、4 月版
GFSRの公表以降、投資適格社債市場全体と比べて約30 ベーシスポイント拡大したという（注 1）。

資料 2 世界の投資適格社債発行額に占めるハイパースケーラーの割合

（出所）IMF「GFSR（2026 年 10 月）」より第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

現在のAI関連企業の資金調達の特徴として、資金と売上が企業間で相互に結びつく循環的な取
引があげられる。具体的には、大手企業が財務基盤の相対的に弱いAI企業に出資し、出資先がそ

### Page 3

3/5 
の資金で大手企業のサービスや半導体を購入する仕組みだ。GFSRによれば、2023 年以降に公表さ
れた 14 件の取引では、株式投資が約3,000 億ドル、関連する債務が約6,500 億ドルにのぼり、将
来の売上に関する契約は約1 兆 4,000 億ドルに達する。こうした取引はうまくいけば、出資先の
企業価値拡大による投資リターンの増加と売上増加が期待できる。一方、投資先の経営が悪化す
れば、投資損失と将来の売上減少という逆回転が生じる。IMFは、こうした取引による投資資金や
将来の売上が少数の企業に集中しており、特定の投資先の経営悪化がAI関連企業の間で損失を広
げるリスクを指摘している。 
AI関連企業に投資する側の懸念としてGFSRが取り上げたのは、株価上昇が続くなか、借り入れ
などを利用した取引が拡大し、投資家が過大なリスクを抱えている可能性だ。ヘッジファンドの
株式ポジションと信用取引融資残高の合計は、2024 年末の先進国GDP比約10％から2026 年には約
15％に増加した。また、レバレッジ型上場投資信託（ETF）に占める個別株を対象としたETFの割
合は、16％から28％に上昇した。GFSRの補足分析によると、株式オプションの価格から推計した
レバレッジ取引の資金調達コストも、2026 年初めからほぼ倍増している（注 2）。レバレッジをか
けた取引は株価が下落した際の損失が大きくなり、追加証拠金を拠出するために他の資産を売ら
ざるを得なくなるといった形で、他の金融商品にも波及する場合がある。

3. 国債利回りの急上昇リスク  
AIと同様に金融市場で注目が高まっている国債について、GFSRは利回りの急上昇リスクを4 月
版から「悪化」と評価した。特に注目したいのは、長期国債を保有するリスクに応じて投資家が
求める上乗せ金利（タームプレミアム）の上昇だ。IMFによると、10 年国債利回りの上昇には将来
の政策金利の予想とタームプレミアムの双方が寄与しており、特に先進国のタームプレミアムは
歴史的な変動範囲の上限に近い水準にある。 
タームプレミアムを押し上げているのは、財政赤字の継続や高水準の政府債務に対する懸念、
今後の国債発行増加の見通しだ。ユーロ圏、英国、米国では、2026 年1～3 月期に投資適格社債の
発行が国債を含む債券発行総額の4 割を占めた（デュレーション加重） 。さらにGFSRは、AI関連企
業による社債発行が急増し、国債と投資家の資金を奪い合うことで、国債市場への追加的な圧力
になっているとの指摘も紹介している。 
IMFが警戒するのは、インフレ再燃などをきっかけにタームプレミアムがさらに上昇する事態
だ。主要国では、政府債務が歴史的に高い水準にあるという問題が未解決のままで、国債の借り
換えリスクも高い。特に、国債の償還年限を短期化した国では、金利が上昇すると高い金利で借
り換える必要が生じ、財政負担が増える。長期債から短期債への置き換えが大規模に進めば、こ
うしたリスクは一段と高まる可能性がある。 
政府債務が増えて国債発行が膨らむと、国内の銀行や保険会社などだけでは増発分を消化しき
れず、外国人投資家や投資ファンドの存在感が高まりやすい。こうした投資家は価格変動に敏感
で、インフレ懸念が強まった際に長期国債の売却が集中する可能性がある。GFSRでは、これをタ
ームプレミアムの上昇を増幅する要因としてあげている。

### Page 4

4/5 
GFSRは、日本国債市場の流動性悪化を指摘している。日本銀行によるイールドカーブ・コント
ロールの終了後、金融政策の正常化が進むなかで利回りの変動が大きくなったことに加え、特に
長期国債で売値と買値の差が拡大している点をあげた。ユーロ圏と英国では流動性を示す指標が
改善し、米国でも足元は良好な状況にあるのとは対照的だ。 
IMFはタームプレミアムが急上昇した場合、AI関連株を含む幅広い資産の価格が下落するほか、
金融取引に利用される国債の担保価値も低下し、金融市場全体に混乱が広がる可能性を警戒して
いる。IMFは世界各国の銀行を対象に、国債利回りの急上昇やAIの生産性向上効果が期待を下回る
ことなどを想定したストレステストを実施した。この結果、世界全体の銀行システムの自己資本
比率は規制上の最低水準を上回るものの、ストレス後の普通株式等Tier1 比率が 7％を下回る銀行
の資産は、世界の銀行資産全体の22％を占める結果になった（注3）。

4. サイバー攻撃のリスク 
GFSRは、三つ目のリスクとしてあげたサイバーリスクについて、「悪化」したと評価した。GFSR
では金融機関が外部の情報技術サービスへの依存を強めている点を問題視した。クラウドサービ
スやソフトウエアを提供する企業の一部に利用が集中し、多くの金融機関が共通の技術基盤を利
用するようになっている。こうした企業が攻撃を受けてサービスを停止すれば、金融機関への影
響が広範に及ぶ可能性がある。さらに、外部委託先のつながりが複雑になり、リスクの全体像を
把握しにくくなっている点も指摘した。 
サイバー攻撃は高度化が進み、日本でも企業の情報流出が相次ぐ。GFSRは、最先端のAIモデル
がネットワーク攻撃の模擬実験で高い能力を示していると指摘した。GFSRによると、悪用が確認
されたソフトウエアなどの脆弱性のうち、公表日以前または公表当日に悪用された割合（ゼロデ
イ比率）は、2026 年の年初来で70％を超え、前年から約20 ポイント上昇した。脆弱性が公表さ
れてから悪用されるまでの時間も短くなっており、ソフトウエアの提供企業が修正プログラムを
用意する余裕は小さくなっている。

5. おわりに 
今回のGFSRからは、AIの急成長に金融市場や金融システムがどこまで対応できるのかという問
題意識が垣間見える。AI関連企業の利益成長が世界的な株高をけん引する一方、巨額の設備投資
に必要な資金を調達するため、社債市場でもその存在感を高め始めた。技術革新が進むほど投資
資金への需要も膨らみ、あらゆる市場から資金を吸い上げる構図だ。 
成長分野に資金が集中し、市場が過熱することは過去にも繰り返されてきた。今回のAIブーム
で特徴的なのは、巨額の資金需要が国債市場の需給にまで影響を及ぼす可能性があることだ。AI
の成長が金融市場を動かす一方、金利の上昇が今度はAI投資の採算を左右する。相互依存の強ま
りが今回のGFSRからも確認できたといえる。 
以 上

なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。
5/5

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
• The recent tendency of some firms to bundle into prices
card-acceptance costs that had previously been surcharged
– and then some, in some cases – demonstrates a ‘cost-
plus’ approach to pricing. To the extent they can pass on
these costs through to prices, it suggests that demand was

strong enough to bear it. The RBA will take this as a signal
that demand is still outstripping supply.
• This is mostly a story about non-labour costs. Base wages
growth has been moderate for some years, though the
above-average increase in award wages this year will add
a little. Not all firms will seek to pass these cost increases
on, but some will, including because they have limited
scope to absorb the cost increase into margins.
• A cost-plus mentality has drawbacks, including that it

limits the imagination away from finding alternative,
efficiency-improving responses. It also accords with
recent academic research suggesting that firms form
expectations and set prices according to what is
happening in their own industry and experience, while
putting little weight on economy-wide inflation or central
bank credibility. This is an unpleasant message for many
consumers or anyone in an interest-sensitive sector when
supply shocks abound.

The recent banning of card surcharges has caused
considerable upset this month, even though it is a reversion
to historical and international practice. The card schemes
used to impose “no-surcharge” rules, until the RBA banned
these in 2003, and many other countries still have them. The
turnaround represents the RBA throwing in the towel on its
longstanding effort to use price incentives to get people to
choose low-cost payment methods. Surcharges no longer

made sense in a largely cashless economy. In any case, least-
cost routing puts the power to choose lower-cost methods in
the hands of the merchants who actually bear the cost.
In most cases, firms will simply roll the surcharge into the sticker
price, leaving the total cost to the consumer unchanged, just
more transparent. We also understand that some large retailers
have absorbed the cost – if they were surcharging in the first
place, which many did not. (The RBA estimated that around

16% of businesses imposed surcharges.) Inevitably, though,
some firms have lifted their prices by more than any reasonable
estimate of the merchant service fee that the surcharge was
meant to cover. And because this is Australia, of course takeaway
coffee has been the centre of consumer discontent.
Higher prices for one’s caffeine fix was unlikely to be the main
instigator of this month’s slump in consumer sentiment. Petrol
‘Cost-Plus’ costs
Forecast changes

• A full update to our forecasts was published in our October
Market Outlook  on Thursday 15 October.
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
prices are typically more salient for cost-of-living concerns, and
the before-and-after picture on the RBA announcement that week
shows that higher interest rates also contributed to the decline.
In some senses, though, the post-surcharge prices and the
RBA’s decisions are linked: businesses passing on increased

costs into their own prices – and expecting to be able to
make that increase stick – is exactly the kind of inflationary
environment the RBA is leaning against. Firms have been
subjected to an array of non-labour cost escalations, from
pandemic-era supply chain issues to electricity to fuel to
water to building supplies. And IT equipment prices, which
for decades were a reliable source of cost reductions, are
nowadays flat to up. Passing these costs on, leaving margins

intact, demonstrates that demand is not weak enough to
prevent this, and signals that firms’ inflationary expectations
might be higher than the RBA desires. The RBA will take this
as a sign that demand is still outstripping supply.
This is mostly a story about non-labour costs. While the Fair
Work Commission did hand down an above-average increase in
award wages this year and this was probably passed through
to prices in hospitality and other labour-intensive sectors, base

wages growth has been moderate for some years. Business
surveys point to non-labour cost escalation being the real issue
lately. This is why we were puzzled by the passage in the RBA
minutes this week suggesting that cost pressures reported
in business surveys might imply that the unemployment rate
consistent with inflation at target (the NAIRU) has risen. It is
a convenient belief, though, if unemployment is starting to
overshoot your forecasts, as the latest labour market data have

done compared with the RBA’s August forecasts.
Not all firms can pass on cost increases. Most small firms do
not have the market power, though coffee may be an exception
because convenience induces a kind of local market power. And
as noted above, large firms with everyday-low-price strategies
will instead try to find savings elsewhere in the supply chain.
Still, some firms will seek to pass these cost increases on.
Some observers might see this as corporate greed, but another

way to frame this strategy is simply as a ‘cost-plus’ mentality.
“Entrenched expectations that cost increases
can and should be passed on are precisely
what tight monetary policy is leaning against,
but only indirectly."

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
It turns out that cost-plus pricing is especially common in
areas where regulators determine the price – water and other
utilities being examples where costs and prices have escalated
a lot in recent years.
There are limits to firms’ ability to absorb costs, too. The

failures of so many building firms a few years ago, squeezed
between post-pandemic cost escalation, delivery delays and
fixed-price contracts, were a case in point. Normally, there is
a contractual response, and it need not end in insolvency. The
episode does illustrate, however, that it is unrealistic to expect
firms never to pass cost increases through to prices.
The cost-plus mentality does have its drawbacks. It limits
the imagination away from finding responses that might save

costs elsewhere to absorb the cost hike, and in so doing, boost
efficiency. It was equally limiting in times of soft demand: in
the years before the pandemic, many firms professed a belief
that, because they couldn’t get price increases to stick, they
could not possibly accept cost increases, including in wages.
Thus, Australia ended up with wages growth too low to be
consistent with inflation at target, and the peculiar spectacle
of a central bank governor complaining that wages growth

was too low. And if labour remains cheap, why invest to
economise on it and thereby boost productivity?
A cost-plus mentality also amplifies the pass-through of
supply shocks. This is not quite the same thing as the ‘inflation
expectations’ seen in standard economic models of inflation,
but it is related. The stronger the tendency towards cost-plus
pricing, the worse the inflation response to a supply shock.
This is also true when inflation expectations are high, but the

distinction matters. The recent academic literature includes
some fascinating empirical evidence  from New Zealand that
suggests that beliefs about current and future overall inflation
have little bearing on many firms’ pricing decisions. As related
work using French data shows, firms’ pricing choices are
shaped less by economy-wide inflation developments than by
what is happening to their costs and in their industry.
That adds up to economy-wide behaviour that looks a lot

like firms (in aggregate) setting prices according to expected
inflation (in aggregate), but under the hood something
quite different is going on. If firms’ beliefs about future
economy-wide inflation do not matter much in their pricing
decisions, then neither does the credibility of a central bank’s
commitment to return inflation to target. The mechanism to
get inflation down then must be simply to weaken demand
enough to convince firms that cost-plus will not fly in the

market. This is an unpleasant message for many consumers
or anyone in an interest-sensitive sector when supply shocks
abound. It does, however, imply that if supply shocks fade
or even self-correct, so will high inflation. If and when that
happens, tight monetary policy can be unwound.
The deeper question is whether cost-plus price dynamics can
be reduced. That is more likely to be a question for regulators
than for central banks.

THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: getting the balance right
bias towards an October or December decision. Williams
stated he believes one more hike would prove sufficient in
this cycle given known information. And Waller also showed
confidence, highlighting that elevated long-term yields are

doing some of the FOMC’s work, restricting demand in interest
rate sensitive sectors. We hold a similar view, anticipating that
one more hike by year end and a little time will bring inflation
near the 2.0%yr medium-term target. However, as discussed
in this month’s Market Outlook , capacity pressures are likely
to see inflation consistently above target, rather than below,
in coming years – particularly if, as we suspect, capacity in the
housing sector remains tight. This will mean the FOMC have to

remain modestly restrictive over the forecast horizon and will
also bias up long-term yields.
In Asia, there was one release that stood out, China’s trade
account. At USD133.5bn, the September result was a whisker
inside January 2025’s record high of USD137.9bn. More
impressively, throughout 2025 and 2026 year-to-date the
surplus has now averaged USD100.9bn, almost 3 times the
average of 2019, prior to the pandemic. The geographic breadth
of export growth is as impressive as the headline annual gain of

36.3%yr, even more so given the ongoing strength in Chinese
investment in neighbouring nations’ production chains. In time,
both trade and income receipts from offshore investment should
buoy the renminbi, although for now continued outbound
foreign investment should moderate the pace of gains against
other nations in the region, and likely versus the US dollar.
Another key trend assessed in October’s Market Outlook is the
evolution of producer prices across Asia, and the likelihood

of the disinflationary benefit the west has received in recent
decades continuing. Historically, labour cost arbitrage was
one of the key drivers of declining goods prices. While
still possible, we expect the next phase of manufacturing
development to instead be driven by capital, technology and
energy. Many end users and the manufacturers undertaking
production are also increasingly prioritising resilience, security
and specialisation over cost minimisation. It is therefore more

probably future supply-chain developments will help contain
rising costs through productivity than drive sustained price
declines for the goods bought by consumers and businesses.
Australia’s key data release this week was the September
Labour Force Survey . Employment surprised to the
downside, –39.3k. However, a 0.2ppt decline in labour force
participation from August’s near-historic high largely offset.
The unemployment rate therefore held at 4.7% after August

was revised up.
Residual seasonality may be in the mix. Regardless,
the take home point from the update is that the labour
market is continuing to ease as employment growth runs
below working-age population growth. We expect the
unemployment rate to grind higher into 2027 as tighter
financial conditions and cost-of-living pressures restrict
activity growth to a below-trend pace. Although, our forecast
peak is near at 4.8%, due by Q2 2027.
This update on the labour market complicates the

communication challenge for the RBA. The September minutes
were firmly on the hawkish side, with the discussion focussing
on materialising upside risks to inflation. But, with the
unemployment rate now through the RBA’s NAIRU assumption
of 4.6%, Governor Bullock’s “a little tight” descriptor for the
labour market is becoming harder to defend. Still, with a clear
focus on inflation risks – which also led us to revise up our
near-term CPI forecasts in the October Market Outlook  – we

still expect a 25bp rate hike at the November meeting.
Over in the US, the headline CPI rose 0.6% as expected in
September, taking the annual rate up to 3.7%yr. Energy prices
were a key contributor (5.2%, 20.4%yr), while core inflation
was a touch above consensus in the month at 0.3% (2.6%yr).
Within the core components, core goods prices gained 0.3%
after being broadly unchanged over the past 6 months
(0.7%yr). Core services meanwhile rose 0.4% (3.1%yr) on a

0.4% increase in shelter (3.2%yr), a 0.6% gain for transport
services and a 0.3% increase in medical care services (2.7%yr
and 2.5%yr respectively). Further, the subsequent release
of the PPI pointed to continued upstream price pressures,
with the headline and core measures up 6.3%yr and 5.0%yr
respectively. The latest Beige Book provided colour on these
developments, firms across multiple districts citing tariffs,
insurance and commodity prices as ongoing sources of cost

pressure. Guiding on the risks, many respondents expect
further price increases as contracts/hedges roll off.
Given their telegraphed concerns over inflation, there is
enough signal in these reports to warrant a follow-up hike
at the October FOMC meeting. Though it seems Committee
members believe they are near the end of the tightening cycle
and so have time on their side. This week, Daly and Bowman
both showed patience in assessing the trend, with no clear

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
Downward revisions to 2027 outlook
This week we have updated our New Zealand economic
outlook for the next year, in particular reflecting the latest
developments in the energy market and key financial
variables (long-term interest rates and the exchange rate).

After last week’s marking down of our forecast for through-
year GDP growth in 2026 to 2.5%yr, this week we have
reduced our forecast of growth in 2027 by 0.3ppts to 2.7%yr.
This downward revision reflects a less positive assessment
of the outlooks for consumption and investment (including
residential investment) considering the more elevated path
assumed for energy prices and a higher near-term track for
long-term interest rates (as discussed in our updated Market

Outlook, published earlier this week).
A partial offset to weaker growth in domestic demand is
provided by a slightly more positive outlook for exports
(particularly exports of manufactured goods and tourism
inflows) because of a downward revision to our expectations
for the NZ dollar. We now expect NZD/USD to end this
year around current levels at 0.56 – 3c lower than expected
previously – before appreciating to around 0.64 over the
course of 2027 as interest rate differentials with the US

gradually close and as the USD appreciates. We think that the
low value of the NZ dollar – in trade-weighted terms now at a
17-year low – helps to explain the recent strong performance
in the previously moribund manufacturing sector, as well as
tourist inflows from key markets such as Australia.
Last week we raised our forecast for CPI inflation at year end
to 4.3%yr, largely on account of recent developments in global
energy prices (although the weak NZ dollar has compounded the

effect). This week we have slightly revised up our forecast for
inflation for the first half of 2027. But because energy prices are
assumed to decline in 2027 from what is now a higher starting
level, our 2027 year-end forecast for inflation has nudged
down 0.1ppts to 1.6%yr. Needless to say, forecasts of headline
inflation, in particular, are subject to significant revision given the
uncertain outlook for the Middle East conflict and uncertainty

about how that will be reflected in global energy prices.
Ahead of next week’s very important Q3 CPI report – which
could prove decisive – we have made no changes to our
forecast for the RBNZ’s policy settings. So, for now we
continue to think that despite the higher near-term inflation
outlook the RBNZ will stick with the strategy communicated
in the September Monetary Policy Statement, i.e. that at the
vv28 October meeting it will pause to assess the impact of this

year’s 50bps of policy tightening, before considering a further
upward adjustment in December.
Turning to this week’s data, the news from the housing market
remained decidedly downbeat. House sales fell a further 4% in
September – the seventh consecutive month of decline – to be
12% lower than a year earlier. With the stock of listings at an
11-year high, the median selling time remains elevated. And
so unsurprisingly, house prices fell for a fourth consecutive

month and are now down 1.6%yr. Prices remain especially
soft in the North Island, with prices down more than 3%yr in
Auckland and more than 4%yr in Wellington. Given upward
pressures on mortgage rates, broader strength in the economy
– including a stronger labour market – will likely be required to
reverse this trend. On that score, MBIE’s measure of online job
advertisements was little changed in September, continuing
the flat trend since the beginning of this year.

In other news, retail spending, as measured by electronic card
payments, decreased 0.4%m/m in September following an
unrevised 0.9% decline in August. This was a weaker result than
we had expected based on the recent sawtooth pattern and
our own internal data. We should treat these data with some
suspicion given that for quite a while now the Stats NZ series
seems not to have reflected the spending seen through banks’
own card spending networks, perhaps suggesting changes in the

payments arena may be leaving the official sector data behind.
Even so, unsurprisingly, with petrol prices rebounding late in
the month, spending on fuel increased 2.9%mth. However,
core spending, which excludes spending on fuel and vehicles,
fell 0.7%m/m. While spending on consumables (groceries)
increased modestly in September, this was at the expense of
spending on ‘nice to haves’. Spending on hospitality suffered a

Senior Economist

have relied on foreign visitors to drive growth in spending.
And the good news is that visitor arrivals increased to a new
post-Covid high in August to be up 8%yr. Arrivals of Chinese
citizens have grown by almost a third to sit near pre-Covid
levels in recent months, while arrivals of US citizens grew

17%yr. However, the Middle East conflict has impacted
long-haul arrivals from Europe (UK arrivals are down 8%yr).
Meanwhile, inflows of migrants remain at modest levels,
totalling around 20,000 in the year to August – about half
what might be expected on average over the cycle.
News from the manufacturing sector remained positive in
September, with the BusinessNZ Manufacturing PMI rising
to a 3-month high of 56.5 in September. Improvement was
seen across all subcomponents of the index, with the new

orders index rising 1.8pts to 57.0. We think that the recovery in
manufacturing this year owes much to the weakness of the NZ
dollar – especially against the Australian dollar – and activity
associated with a strong domestic primary sector and an
emerging recovery in domestic residential construction activity.
Last but certainly not least, ahead of next week’s all-important
Q3 CPI, the Selected Prices report for September was firmer
than expected. That was mainly due to larger than expected

increases in airfares and holiday accommodation costs, which
offset softer than expected food prices.
Accounting for the September prices data, we’ve left our
forecast for Q3 inflation  at 0.7%. That would see the annual
inflation rate dropping back to 3.7%, down from 4.1% in the
year to June. Underlying that move has been a moderation in
fuel prices after they rocketed higher in the wake of the Middle
East war. That’s balanced against increases in council rates,

building costs and food prices.
Our forecast is lower than the RBNZ’s September MPS
forecast for a 0.8% rise (+3.9%yr). However, with some large
swings in volatile items like fuel, the RBNZ will likely focus
on measures of core inflation. Core inflation was already at
firm levels in the June quarter, with key measures sitting in
the upper part of the RBNZ’s target band. We expect that
they will take another step higher in September, to sit around

3%. Upside surprises in core inflation would be an important
development for the RBNZ ahead of the October OCR review,
as this could suggest that the lower NZD and higher oil prices
are boosting underlying price pressures by more and sooner
than they may have expected.

### Page 7

Q3 is likely to produce a very similar outcome to Q2, with 
quarterly growth of around 1.0% holding the annual rate at 
4.3%yr. Year-to-date, growth will remain around 4.7%ytd 
thanks to the Q1 result.
The detail and partial data for September will again highlight 
the weak state of the consumer and domestic-oriented 
businesses, but also the incredible strength of exports. With 
the trade surplus back at record highs, there are limits to the 
contribution possible from net exports hence, necessitating 
a strengthening of domestic demand if aggregate growth is 
to continue at the rate authorities intend into 2027. Expect 
additional stimulus through the turn of the year.

Definition
Business bankruptcies
Banque de France
Monthly
Companies subject to collective proceedings (receivership and judicial liquidation). Monthly data from the
Banque de France aggregated over a quarter to minimise the risk of calendar effects
Business creations
Insee
Monthly
Survey of business creations (all types, companies, sole traders, micro-entrepreneurs). Monthly data from
INSEE aggregated over a quarter to minimise the risk of calendar effects

Non performing loans ratio
EBA
Quarterly
Gross non performing loans and advances at amortised cost / Total gross loans and advances at
amortised cost
Share of underperforming loans (Stage 2 IFRS 9)
BCE
Quarterly
Loans and advances in Stage 2 / Total loans and advances. Loans and advances at amortised cost and at
fair value through equity
Economic weight of bankruptcies
Banque de France
Monthly
Total outstanding bank loans to newly bankrupt NFCs over a one-year period, calculated as the figure for

the month in which the firm is declared bankrupt, relative to the average outstanding bank loans to both
healthy and bankrupt NFCs during the same period.
Share of 90+ days past due payments
URSSAF
Monthly
Contributions still to be collected by Urssaf / Amount of contributions due to Urssaf

Document 241: COLA comfort for older consumers_ Older households’ spending has lagged, but the 2027 Social Security cost-of-living adjustment (COLA) could help narrow the gap. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/COLA comfort for older consumers_ Older households’ spending has lagged, but the 2027 Social Security cost-of-living adjustment (COLA) could help narrow the gap. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

13034011     1

Economy 
COLA comfort for older consumers? 
27 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Older generations' spending growth has lagged other cohorts over the last year in Bank of America aggregated credit and debit 
card data, despite sizeable wealth gains. This likely partly reflects the highly uneven distribution of wealth among older 
households, limiting the extent to which rising wealth supports spending across the cohort. 
• More recently, stronger consumer spending appears to be primarily a labor-income story. However, older households are less 
exposed to accelerating wage growth. In addition, large differences in their income sources help explain the considerable 
variation in spending among this cohort. 
• The recently announced 3.6% Social Security cost-of-living adjustment could provide a meaningful boost in 2027. If inflation 
moderates as expected, benefit increases may outpace price growth, supporting older households' purchasing power and 
potentially narrowing their spending gap with other generations.

Older generations’ spending growth has lagged  
While total credit and debit card spending per household has picked up in 2026 in Bank of America internal data, a notable 
laggard has been the spending growth of older generations (Baby Boomers and Traditionalists). For around a year, discretionary 
spending growth for these older generations has been lower than that of overall households. This is in stark contrast to 2022-
2023, when they were significantly outstripping overall household spending growth (Exhibit 1).  
Exhibit 1: Discretionary spending growth has been slower among older (Baby Boomer and Traditionalist) households despite wealth effects 
Total credit and debit card spending per household on discretionary categories by older generations and overall (three-month average, % YoY)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

-4%
0%
4%
8%
12%
16%
20%
2022 2023 2024 2025 2026
Spending growth differential (pp) Overall Baby Boomer and Traditionalist
I NSTI TUTE
Accessible version

### Page 2

2 27 October 2026 
  I NSTI TUTE

On one level, this observation seems surprising given that in our piece on the older consumer we noted that a large and growing 
proportion of net worth is held by older cohorts (read: Entering the age of the older consumer). So what’s going on? One 
explanation is that wealth is very unevenly distributed even within the older cohort. Exhibit 2 uses the latest Federal Reserve 
(2025) Survey of Consumer Finances to illustrate that, across households older than 65 years of age, around two-thirds of net 
wealth is held by the wealthiest 10% of households. As a result, wealth gains from rising stock markets may not trickle too far 
down the wealth distribution of older consumers.  
Exhibit 2: The wealth distribution for older households is very skewed  
Distribution of net wealth for households over 65 years old (%)

Source: Federal Reserve Board of Governors (2025 Survey of Consumer Finances)  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Income and spending become more diverse and unequal with age 
Another important part of the story is that the rise in overall spending growth over the past year appears to be supported by 
labor market strength as well as rising wealth. The October Consumer Checkpoint described how a pickup in after-tax wage 
growth among lower- and middle-income households was likely a key factor driving a recovery in these cohorts’ spending growth 
(read: Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers). As households age, their income sources become more diverse (Exhibit 3), 
which means older generations will likely benefit less from this pickup in wage growth.  
Exhibit 3: As households age their sources of income become more diverse  
Sources of income by age (%)

Source: Federal Reserve Board of Governors (2025 Survey of Household Economics and Decision making) 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

spending. For example, using Bank of America internal data, Exhibit 4 shows that households over age 62 that rely solely on

The 2027 COLA may offer some respite  
Still in our view there are some reasons for optimism around older generations spending outcomes.  
For some older households in the middle of the income distribution, tax policy is likely providing some support to their spending 
growth. The One Big Beautiful Bill Act introduced a temporary deduction of up to $6,000 for each taxpayer aged 65 or older for 
2025-2028, with the benefit phasing out above $75,000 of modified adjusted gross income for single filers and $150,000 for 
joint filers. This measure is likely lifting after-tax income and spending capacity for older households – particularly those in the 
middle of the income distribution.  
However, households on lower incomes may see less benefit from these policies as they likely already owe limited federal 
income tax. For these households relying on Social Security either as their sole or as a large component of their income, the 
forthcoming annual cost-of-living adjustment (COLA) to social security benefits will likely be a more important determinant of 
their budgets for 2027. The announced COLA for 2027 is a rise of 3.6% (Exhibit 7) – the largest since 2023, though lower than 
the 8.7% rise in 2022. 
Exhibit 7: The 2027 COLA increase is the largest since 2023 and close to the historical average  
Social Security cost of living adjustments (% increase as of January effective date)

Source: US Social Security Administration (SSA)   
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
The COLA increases are based on the year-over-year (YoY) % change between the third quarters of successive years in the 
Consumer Price Index for Urban Wage Earners and Clerical Workers (CPI-W) and are typically announced in October and effective 
the following January. If the COLA increase simply keeps benefits in line with inflation, why is it particularly interesting? For sure, 
many times it is not. But when inflation is close to a turning point, the increase can end up looking relatively high or low 
compared with inflation over the course of the following year. The 2022 COLA increase was a case in point, as inflation in the 
subsequent year slowed sharply. In our view, this was one reason older generations’ spending growth was stronger than that of 
other age cohorts over 2022-2023.   
Exhibit 8: Inflation is expected to decline next year 
Consumer Price Index (CPI) (% YoY), third quarter CPI-W increases (red dots, % YoY) and latest consensus CPI forecast for 2027 (dotted line, % YoY)

Source: Haver Analytics, Bloomberg. Some fall 2025 months might be missing due to the government shutdown.

Regarding the 2027 COLA announcement, there are some parallels with 2022, albeit on a smaller scale. In particular, the 
consensus expectation is that CPI inflation is likely to cool over 2027, largely reflecting the lessening impact of higher gasoline 
prices (Exhibit 8).  
So if these expectations prove correct, households that rely on Social Security may receive a more impactful boost to their 
budgets next year than they have for the last few, potentially narrowing the gap between older generations’ spending and other 
households.  
What categories of spending growth might benefit from this? It is hard to be sure, but notable growth deficits for older 
generations currently reside in discretionary areas like electronics, lodging and restaurants (Exhibit 9). Some of these may reflect 
the preferences of older households, but it could be that there is room for some narrowing in these areas.

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
Store category aggregation for “more discretionary”, “partly discretionary”, and “mostly necessity” is defined as follows: 
More discretionary: furniture, electronics, building materials, hobbies, miscellaneous stores, nonstore retail, clothing. 
Partly discretionary: auto, health and personal care, general merchandise. 
Mostly necessity: Gasoline, food and beverage.  
We consider a measure of services necessity spending that includes but is not limited to groceries, utilities, and gasoline. Discretionary services 
includes but is not limited to charitable donations, leisure travel, entertainment, and professional/consumer services. Discretionary retail 
includes but is not limited to general merchandise, miscellaneous, clothing, electronics and furniture. It excludes categories like groceries and 
gasoline. Holiday spending is defined as items in which spending in the November-December period is usually at least 20% of total annual 
spending on the category.  Value and premium grocers are determined judgmentally on a proprietary analysis of merchants by equity analysts. 
The “more discretionary” retail category includes furniture, electronics, building materials, online marketplaces, clothing retailers and 
miscellaneous stores; while “partly discretionary” retail contains auto parts and services, health and personal care retailers, general merchandise 
and “mostly necessity”  retail contains gas and groceries. 
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Bank of America Proprietary Market Landscape Insights Study is an online quantitative survey among Bank of America customers and 
noncustomers sampled and balanced to provide a representative view of the U.S. adult population. Insights are based on aggregated and 
anonymized responses to surveys. Significance testing is done at the 90 percent confidence interval. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026
